These observations highlight critical gaps in LLM capabilities and provide concrete directions for improvement, including techniques addressing sparse reward landscapes and enhanced domain specialization. The creation of the FormalMATH-Lite subset is an important addition.

**Strengths:**

*   **Large and diverse dataset:** FormalMATH provides a more comprehensive evaluation of LLM reasoning capabilities than existing benchmarks.
*   **Human-in-the-loop pipeline:** The autoformalization pipeline significantly reduces manual annotation effort while maintaining data fidelity.
*   **Detailed evaluation of LLMs:** The paper provides valuable insights into the limitations and biases of current LLM-based theorem provers.
*   **Challenging Benchmark:** FormalMATH appears to present genuine challenges to current best-in-class LLMs.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited exploration of the pipeline:** While the autoformalization pipeline is a significant contribution, the paper focuses mainly on the end result of the dataset and evaluation. More detailed analysis of the individual components of the pipeline (e.g., the performance of different LLMs in the semantic verification step, the effectiveness of the negation-based filtering) would enhance the paper.
*   **Lack of ablations on prompt engineering in autoformalization:** A broader ablation study on autoformalization prompt engineering would further enhance the study.

**Justification for Score:**

This paper makes a significant contribution to the field by providing a large-scale and challenging benchmark for formal mathematical reasoning. The thorough evaluation of existing LLMs on FormalMATH reveals important limitations and guides future research directions. However, there's room to explore the analysis further by analyzing components of the pipeline in more detail. It offers meaningful insights and serves as a strong foundation for future research.

### Knowledge Graphs for Enhancing Large Language Models in Entity Disambiguation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02737v2)
- **Authors**: Gerard Pons, Besim Bilalli, Anna Queralt
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in Large Language Models (LLMs) have positioned them as a prominent solution for Natural Language Processing tasks. Notably, they can approach these problems in a zero or few-shot manner, thereby eliminating the need for training or fine-tuning task-specific models. However, LLMs face some challenges, including hallucination and the presence of outdated knowledge or missing information from specific domains in the training data. These problems cannot be easily solved by retraining the models with new data as it is a time-consuming and expensive process. To mitigate these issues, Knowledge Graphs (KGs) have been proposed as a structured external source of information to enrich LLMs. With this idea, in this work we use KGs to enhance LLMs for zero-shot Entity Disambiguation (ED). For that purpose, we leverage the hierarchical representation of the entities' classes in a KG to gradually prune the candidate space as well as the entities' descriptions to enrich the input prompt with additional factual knowledge. Our evaluation on popular ED datasets shows that the proposed method outperforms non-enhanced and description-only enhanced LLMs, and has a higher degree of adaptability than task-specific models. Furthermore, we conduct an error analysis and discuss the impact of the leveraged KG's semantic expressivity on the ED performance.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, with a score and detailed justification.

**Summary:**

The paper presents a method to enhance Large Language Models (LLMs) for the Entity Disambiguation (ED) task using Knowledge Graphs (KGs). The core idea is to leverage the hierarchical class structure within KGs to guide the LLM in its disambiguation process.  The method works in two main steps: (1) generating a subgraph from the KG containing candidate entities and their class taxonomy, and (2) iteratively pruning the subgraph based on the LLM's responses to prompts asking about the entity's class or identity, using the entity descriptions to disambiguate when needed. The authors compare their approach against a plain LLM baseline, ChatEL (another LLM prompting approach), and ReFinED (a task-specific, trained model). The results show improvements over the LLM baselines and increased robustness compared to ReFinED for unseen domains.  The paper also investigates the impact of KG semantic expressivity (using YAGO vs. DBpedia) and performs error analysis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using KGs to guide LLMs in ED is not entirely novel. Several prior works augment LLMs with external knowledge, including entity descriptions, types, or KG triples. However, the specific approach of using the class *hierarchy* within a KG for *iterative pruning* is relatively novel. This provides a structured way to constrain the LLM's search space and prevent it from hallucinating or relying on potentially incorrect general knowledge.  It builds upon existing Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) techniques by employing a hierarchical KG as the retrieval mechanism.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in demonstrating a relatively simple, yet effective, method for improving LLM performance in ED without the need for task-specific training or fine-tuning. This makes the approach more flexible and adaptable to new domains or entities compared to trained models like ReFinED. Showing the improvements with a relatively less powerful LLM (GPT-3.5) compared to ChatEL, which uses GPT-4, is also a significant result. Further, the analysis of KG expressivity offers insights into how the quality and granularity of the KG can impact ED performance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear and well-defined method:** The algorithm is clearly explained and relatively easy to implement.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper includes a good range of datasets and comparisons against relevant baselines.
    *   **Insightful error analysis:**  The categorization of errors provides valuable insights into the limitations of the approach and directions for future research.
    *   **Analysis of KG impact:**  The investigation of KG expressivity adds depth to the analysis and provides practical guidance on selecting appropriate KGs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited KG scope:** The evaluation primarily focuses on YAGO and DBpedia, which are both derived from Wikipedia. It would be valuable to see how the method performs with other KGs that have different characteristics or are specific to certain domains (e.g., biomedical KGs).
    *   **Relying on Candidate Sets:** The method depends on an initial set of candidate entities, which might limit performance if the correct entity is not included. The method is thus not truly end-to-end, even though the prompt includes all the candidate information needed for the LLM to solve the task.
    *   **GPT-3.5 usage:** While using GPT-3.5 might be a cost-effective decision, the results may be even better with a stronger LLM like GPT-4 or Mistral Large, or a LLM specialized in knowledge retrieval.  A comparison using similar budget would have been a useful experiment.
    *   **Simplistic Prompt Engineering:** The paper relies on rather simplistic prompt engineering. More advanced prompt engineering, or even a few-shot example, could result in better results, especially in the baseline scenarios.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Reasoning Chains:** Although the paper leverages KGs to enhance reasoning in entity disambiguation, it does not fully explore the potential of reasoning chains or inference paths within the KGs to improve the accuracy of disambiguation.
    *   **No comparison to fully open ED (zero candidate list).** The comparison against a baseline where the candidate list is not used would have been interesting. The authors could have measured how often the correct label is amongst the labels mentioned in the LLM description.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by demonstrating a practical approach for enhancing LLMs with structured knowledge for ED. It highlights the importance of KG structure and semantic expressivity and encourages further research into KG-enhanced LLMs for NLP tasks.  The method is relatively easy to implement and adapt, which could lead to its adoption in various applications.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is a solid contribution with clear novelty, significant results, and insightful analysis. However, the limitations mentioned above prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking work. While the idea of using KG hierarchies is clever, the overall method builds upon existing techniques. The limitations of the KGs explored, reliance on candidate sets, and GPT 3.5 use case limits the performance, even though it provides useful improvements. The error analysis helps understand the approach, however more LLM error exploration would have been useful.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Advancing Generalizable Tumor Segmentation with Anomaly-Aware Open-Vocabulary Attention Maps and Frozen Foundation Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02753v1)
- **Authors**: Yankai Jiang, Peng Zhang, Donglin Yang, Yuan Tian, Hai Lin, Xiaosong Wang
- **Abstract**: We explore Generalizable Tumor Segmentation, aiming to train a single model for zero-shot tumor segmentation across diverse anatomical regions. Existing methods face limitations related to segmentation quality, scalability, and the range of applicable imaging modalities. In this paper, we uncover the potential of the internal representations within frozen medical foundation diffusion models as highly efficient zero-shot learners for tumor segmentation by introducing a novel framework named DiffuGTS. DiffuGTS creates anomaly-aware open-vocabulary attention maps based on text prompts to enable generalizable anomaly segmentation without being restricted by a predefined training category list. To further improve and refine anomaly segmentation masks, DiffuGTS leverages the diffusion model, transforming pathological regions into high-quality pseudo-healthy counterparts through latent space inpainting, and applies a novel pixel-level and feature-level residual learning approach, resulting in segmentation masks with significantly enhanced quality and generalization. Comprehensive experiments on four datasets and seven tumor categories demonstrate the superior performance of our method, surpassing current state-of-the-art models across multiple zero-shot settings. Codes are available at https://github.com/Yankai96/DiffuGTS.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the provided research paper.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DiffuGTS, a novel framework for generalizable tumor segmentation (GTS). It leverages frozen medical foundation diffusion models (MFDMs) to achieve zero-shot tumor segmentation across diverse anatomical regions and imaging modalities. DiffuGTS creates anomaly-aware open-vocabulary attention (AOVA) maps based on text prompts to guide the segmentation without being limited to a pre-defined category list. To further refine the segmentation masks, it employs a diffusion model-based inpainting technique to transform pathological regions into pseudo-healthy counterparts. Finally, a pixel-level and feature-level residual learning approach is applied to enhance the segmentation quality and generalization. The paper presents comprehensive experiments on four datasets and seven tumor categories, demonstrating the superior performance of DiffuGTS compared to existing state-of-the-art methods across multiple zero-shot settings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a relatively novel approach by combining several techniques in a specific way for the GTS task.
*   The use of a *frozen* pre-trained MFDMs is a clever way to leverage existing knowledge. This is particularly beneficial in the medical domain where data annotation is costly and difficult.
*   The creation of AOVA maps based on *open-vocabulary* text prompts offers flexibility and generalizability not found in traditional methods.
*   The combination of diffusion model inpainting and residual learning adds to the originality. The diffusion model inpainting is used to create pseudo-healthy images, facilitating a more accurate segmentation.
*   The hybrid approach of using both pixel and feature-level residual learning for refining the segmentation mask adds another layer of sophistication.

However, the novelty is also incremental. The paper utilizes several existing techniques (diffusion models, attention mechanisms, residual learning), and combines them.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in medical image analysis: the limited generalizability of tumor segmentation models. By achieving strong zero-shot performance across diverse anatomical regions and imaging modalities, DiffuGTS can have a significant impact in real-world clinical scenarios where training data for specific tumor types may be scarce.

*   **Impact:** A successful GTS system reduces the need for extensive annotation efforts. It can aid clinicians in diagnosis and treatment planning, even for rare or previously unseen tumor types. The quantitative results demonstrate a substantial improvement over existing methods, suggesting a tangible benefit.
*   **Generalizability:** While the paper showcases impressive results on the tested datasets, further validation on even broader and more diverse datasets is crucial to fully assess the generalizability of DiffuGTS in truly real-world clinical settings.
*   **Practical Considerations:** The method uses MFDMs which can be computationally expensive.  The paper acknowledges this limitation and discusses potential avenues for future work.

**Strengths:**

*   Clear problem definition and motivation
*   Well-defined methodology with multiple innovative components
*   Comprehensive experimental evaluation on multiple datasets
*   Strong quantitative results demonstrating superior performance
*   Detailed ablation studies validating the effectiveness of different components
*   Qualitative results that visually illustrate the improvements

**Weaknesses:**

*   Computational cost associated with diffusion models
*   Dependence on the performance of the underlying pre-trained MAISI model.
*   The novelty, while present, is incremental as it builds upon existing techniques.
*   Potential for data leakage from pre-trained MAISI model during training. The authors addressed this concern, but it is still a point of potential risk.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7** to this paper. While the paper builds upon existing techniques, the specific combination and adaptation of these techniques for the GTS task, especially with the emphasis on zero-shot learning and generalizability across modalities, is quite compelling.  The comprehensive experiments and clear gains over the state-of-the-art strengthen the value of this research. The acknowledgment of limitations (computational cost, dependence on MAISI) is also a plus. A slightly higher score could have been justified with a more radical departure from existing methods, but its practical value (better segmentation using a novel framework) is hard to ignore.

### Bye-bye, Bluebook? Automating Legal Procedure with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02763v1)
- **Authors**: Matthew Dahl
- **Abstract**: Legal practice requires careful adherence to procedural rules. In the United States, few are more complex than those found in The Bluebook: A Uniform System of Citation. Compliance with this system's 500+ pages of byzantine formatting instructions is the raison d'etre of thousands of student law review editors and the bete noire of lawyers everywhere. To evaluate whether large language models (LLMs) are able to adhere to the procedures of such a complicated system, we construct an original dataset of 866 Bluebook tasks and test flagship LLMs from OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, Meta, and DeepSeek. We show (1) that these models produce fully compliant Bluebook citations only 69%-74% of the time and (2) that in-context learning on the Bluebook's underlying system of rules raises accuracy only to 77%. These results caution against using off-the-shelf LLMs to automate aspects of the law where fidelity to procedure is paramount.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Bye-bye, Bluebook? Automating Legal Procedure with Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to automate compliance with The Bluebook: A Uniform System of Citation, a complex and detailed manual for legal citation in the United States. The authors construct a novel dataset of 866 Bluebook tasks and evaluate the performance of several flagship LLMs (GPT-4.1, Claude 3.5 Sonnet, Gemini 2.5 Flash, Llama 3.1 405B, and DeepSeek V3 0324) in zero-shot and in-context learning settings. The results indicate that LLMs struggle to consistently produce fully compliant Bluebook citations, even with in-context learning of the underlying rules. The paper concludes that lawyers should be cautious about relying on currently available LLMs to automate procedural aspects of law requiring strict adherence to complex rules.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper is novel in its focus on procedural legal tasks. Existing work largely examines LLMs' capabilities in substantive legal reasoning and information retrieval. Evaluating adherence to detailed style guides like the Bluebook is a niche but important area. The creation of a dedicated Bluebook dataset is a valuable contribution, enabling future research.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from highlighting the limitations of current LLMs in tasks demanding strict adherence to complex, formal rules. While LLMs are making inroads in various domains, the legal field, with its high stakes for procedural accuracy, requires careful evaluation. The paper provides empirical evidence that off-the-shelf LLMs are not yet reliable for automating tasks where fidelity to procedure is paramount.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined research question:** The paper clearly articulates its focus on automating legal procedure.
    *   **Novel dataset:** The creation of a Bluebook task dataset fills a gap in the existing literature.
    *   **Rigorous methodology:** The evaluation is based on exact string matching with expert-verified answers, providing a clear metric. The use of both zero-shot and in-context learning settings allows for a nuanced assessment.
    *   **Error analysis:** The qualitative error analysis highlights the types of mistakes LLMs make, revealing limitations beyond simple stylistic errors.
    *   **Consideration of memorization:** The analysis of case-law performance controlling for memorization is a valuable step towards isolating true rule-following abilities.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited scope:** The focus on the Bluebook, while justified by its complexity, limits the generalizability of the findings to other legal procedures. However, it is a strong starting point.
    *   **No fine-tuning:** While the paper mentions few-shot prompting and fine-tuning, it focuses primarily on zero-shot and in-context learning. Exploring the potential of fine-tuning smaller models on the Bluebook data would have been informative.
    *   **Narrow definition of correctness**: The stringent requirements of 'exact string matching' might be overly critical, as a citation could be functionally correct (e.g., leading to the correct case and page) but still marked wrong if the formatting has a small deviation. This is acknowledged by the authors, but relaxing this constraint and evaluating human-assessed functional correctness would give additional insights.
    *   **Limited evaluation with in-context learning:** Only Gemini 2.5 flash model has been tested for in-context learning.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper may influence the development of specialized LLMs or fine-tuning strategies specifically tailored for legal procedure. It encourages caution and suggests a need for robust validation and human oversight when using LLMs in legal settings. The created Bluebook dataset can serve as a benchmark for future research.
* **Justification:** The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by empirically demonstrating that current LLMs are not yet reliable for tasks requiring strict adherence to complex procedural rules. This is particularly important in the legal domain, where even small errors can have significant consequences. The paper's novel dataset and rigorous methodology provide a solid foundation for future research in this area. It's limitations like the narrow definition of correctness and lack of few-shot testing means that the paper has a smaller influence than it could.

### Giving Simulated Cells a Voice: Evolving Prompt-to-Intervention Models for Cellular Control
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02766v1)
- **Authors**: Nam H. Le, Patrick Erikson, Yanbo Zhang, Michael Levin, Josh Bongard
- **Abstract**: Guiding biological systems toward desired states, such as morphogenetic outcomes, remains a fundamental challenge with far-reaching implications for medicine and synthetic biology. While large language models (LLMs) have enabled natural language as an interface for interpretable control in AI systems, their use as mediators for steering biological or cellular dynamics remains largely unexplored. In this work, we present a functional pipeline that translates natural language prompts into spatial vector fields capable of directing simulated cellular collectives. Our approach combines a large language model with an evolvable neural controller (Prompt-to-Intervention, or P2I), optimized via evolutionary strategies to generate behaviors such as clustering or scattering in a simulated 2D environment. We demonstrate that even with constrained vocabulary and simplified cell models, evolved P2I networks can successfully align cellular dynamics with user-defined goals expressed in plain language. This work offers a complete loop from language input to simulated bioelectric-like intervention to behavioral output, providing a foundation for future systems capable of natural language-driven cellular control.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GIVING SIMULATED CELLS A VOICE: EVOLVING PROMPT-TO-INTERVENTION MODELS FOR CELLULAR CONTROL."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a framework called ZapGPT for controlling simulated cellular dynamics using natural language prompts.  It combines a Large Language Model (LLM) to translate prompts into spatial interventions (Prompt-to-Intervention or P2I model) and a vision-language model (VLM) to interpret the resulting dynamics back into natural language (Dynamics-to-Response or D2R model). The system is trained using evolutionary algorithms to optimize the P2I model based on the alignment between the initial prompt and the system's resulting behavior, focusing on simple tasks like clustering and scattering in a 2D simulation. The paper demonstrates a functional closed-loop system from language input to bioelectric-like intervention to behavioral output.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using natural language to control biological systems is interesting, and the integration of LLMs, VLMs, and evolutionary algorithms in a single framework is novel.  While individual components are well-established, their combination in this specific application, particularly for bio-inspired control, represents a new contribution. The specific P2I and D2R architecture tailored to this task appears to be a contribution, even though the underlying models are not new themselves.

*   **Significance:** The paper offers a potentially significant step towards a more intuitive and accessible way to control biological systems.  Traditionally, biological control involves complex molecular manipulations or hard-coded rules. ZapGPT offers a more abstract, high-level interface. If scalable, this approach could have implications for regenerative medicine, synthetic biology, and other areas where precise control over cellular behavior is critical.
    However, the impact is limited by the simulation environment. The move to *in vitro* or *in vivo* models will be necessary to assess the true value of the proposal.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear and Well-Defined Framework:** The ZapGPT pipeline is well-articulated, with each component (P2I, D2R, evolutionary optimization) clearly explained.
    *   **Proof-of-Concept Demonstration:** The paper provides a functional proof-of-concept, demonstrating that the framework can successfully align cellular dynamics with user-defined goals expressed in plain language (within the simulation).
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The use of various vector field configurations and statistical testing adds rigor to the evaluation. The ablation study comparing the different algorithms is also welcome.
    *   **Identifies Limitations and Future Directions:** The authors acknowledge the limitations of their current implementation (simplified environment, limited vocabulary, interference when training multiple goals) and propose concrete future directions for improvement, such as exploring modular architectures, prompt conditioning, and more flexible evaluation metrics.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Simplified Simulation Environment:** The major limitation is the reliance on a simplified 2D simulation. The gap between this simulation and real biological systems is vast. The validity of the proposed concept depends on its scalability and applicability to *in vitro* or *in vivo* experiments.
    *   **Limited Scope of Interventions and Outcomes:**  The focus on clustering and scattering, while useful for initial validation, is limiting.  Demonstrating control over more complex and biologically relevant behaviors is essential.
    *   **Black Box Nature:**  While the framework aims for interpretable control, the P2I model itself remains a "black box." Understanding how specific prompts translate into specific interventions is difficult. The current implementation offers limited insight into the underlying mechanisms.
    *   **Dependency on Pre-trained Models:** The reliance on pre-trained LLMs and VLMs means that the system inherits their biases and limitations. Furthermore, the VLM output is severely restricted to one-word responses, limiting its applicability in richer scenarios.
    *   **Modest Quantitative Results:** The performance improvements shown through evolutionary optimization are evident but not revolutionary.  The statistical significance is good, but the absolute improvements are moderate.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The ZapGPT framework, even in its current simplified form, could inspire new approaches to bio-inspired control.
    *   The integration of LLMs and VLMs into a closed-loop system for controlling dynamic systems could influence research in robotics, swarm intelligence, and other areas.
    *   The use of evolutionary algorithms to optimize the mapping between natural language prompts and interventions could be a valuable approach for tackling complex control problems.

**Justification for the Score:**

Based on the above evaluation, the paper is assigned a **Score: 6**.

*   It presents a novel and potentially significant idea but is limited by its implementation in a simplified simulation environment. The combination of LLMs, VLMs, and evolutionary algorithms for cellular control is innovative, but the limited scope of interventions, outcomes, and validation on real biological systems significantly restricts its current impact.  The authors clearly acknowledge these limitations and propose concrete future directions, suggesting the potential for future advancements.

Therefore, while the paper demonstrates a functional proof-of-concept with a novel approach, its true value and influence are contingent on its ability to scale and generalize to more complex and realistic scenarios.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### HSplitLoRA: A Heterogeneous Split Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning Framework for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02795v1)
- **Authors**: Zheng Lin, Yuxin Zhang, Zhe Chen, Zihan Fang, Xianhao Chen, Praneeth Vepakomma, Wei Ni, Jun Luo, Yue Gao
- **Abstract**: Recently, large language models (LLMs) have achieved remarkable breakthroughs, revolutionizing the natural language processing domain and beyond. Due to immense parameter sizes, fine-tuning these models with private data for diverse downstream tasks has become mainstream. Though federated learning (FL) offers a promising solution for fine-tuning LLMs without sharing raw data, substantial computing costs hinder its democratization. Moreover, in real-world scenarios, private client devices often possess heterogeneous computing resources, further complicating LLM fine-tuning. To combat these challenges, we propose HSplitLoRA, a heterogeneous parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) framework built on split learning (SL) and low-rank adaptation (LoRA) fine-tuning, for efficiently fine-tuning LLMs on heterogeneous client devices. HSplitLoRA first identifies important weights based on their contributions to LLM training. It then dynamically configures the decomposition ranks of LoRA adapters for selected weights and determines the model split point according to varying computing budgets of client devices. Finally, a noise-free adapter aggregation mechanism is devised to support heterogeneous adapter aggregation without introducing noise. Extensive experiments demonstrate that HSplitLoRA outperforms state-of-the-art benchmarks in training accuracy and convergence speed.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "HSplitLoRA: A Heterogeneous Split Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning Framework for Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HSplitLoRA, a novel framework for fine-tuning Large Language Models (LLMs) on resource-constrained and heterogeneous client devices. HSplitLoRA combines split learning (SL) with Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) for parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT). The framework addresses the challenges posed by limited computing resources, device unavailability due to heterogeneity, and the need for consistent model aggregation across diverse client devices. HSplitLoRA employs three key components: (1) an important weight identification scheme to prioritize trainable weights, (2) an adaptive rank and model splitting configuration to dynamically adjust LoRA ranks and the model split point based on client device capabilities, and (3) a noise-free adapter aggregation mechanism to concatenate low-rank decomposition matrices and maintain consistency across heterogeneous LoRA adapters. Experimental results demonstrate that HSplitLoRA outperforms existing benchmarks in terms of training accuracy and convergence speed.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a relatively novel approach by integrating split learning with LoRA and introducing several innovative components to address heterogeneity in client devices. The key contributions are:

*   **Heterogeneous LLM Fine-tuning Framework:** The first heterogeneous LLM fine-tuning framework that integrates SL and PEFT. This is a significant step towards enabling distributed collaborative learning across heterogeneous client devices.
*   **Important Weight Identification Scheme:** A weight identification scheme to select the important weights for PEFT. Prioritizing weights to fine-tune can improve training time.
*   **Adaptive Rank and Model Splitting Configuration:**  The method of dynamically adjusting LoRA ranks and model split point based on device capabilities is a crucial innovation for handling heterogeneity. This approach is tailored for fine-tuning under resource constraints.
*   **Noise-Free Adapter Aggregation:** This is a crucial aspect that helps to aggregate heterogeneous LoRA adapters without introducing additional noise.

**Significance:** The paper tackles a significant problem: democratizing LLM fine-tuning by making it accessible on resource-constrained devices. The proposed framework has the potential to:

*   **Enable wider participation:** Allow more users with diverse hardware to contribute to and benefit from LLM fine-tuning.
*   **Improve data privacy:** Leverage SL to maintain data privacy during fine-tuning.
*   **Increase efficiency:** Reduce training time and resource consumption through PEFT and intelligent resource allocation.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive approach:** The paper addresses multiple challenges in a unified framework.
*   **Well-designed components:** The individual components (weight identification, adaptive configuration, aggregation) are meticulously designed and justified.
*   **Strong experimental results:** The results demonstrate significant improvements over existing methods.
*   **Clear writing:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited evaluation scenarios:** While the paper evaluates performance with varying devices, the client heterogeneity scenario could be more complex, for example, considering network bandwidth.
*   **Scalability:** While the paper demonstrates an effective approach, the scalability of HSplitLoRA to a very large number of clients needs further consideration.
*   **Synchronous mode:** The assumption of synchronous mode restricts its applicability in realistic distributed settings with unstable connections and varying client availability.

**Justification for the score:**

HSplitLoRA makes a valuable contribution to the field of distributed LLM fine-tuning by addressing the challenges associated with heterogeneous client devices. It introduces effective and well-designed components that offer practical benefits. The paper has some limitations regarding evaluation and scalability, but the approach presented is novel and has the potential to significantly impact the development and deployment of LLMs in real-world scenarios.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Generating HomeAssistant Automations Using an LLM-based Chatbot
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02802v1)
- **Authors**: Mathyas Giudici, Alessandro Sironi, Ismaele Villa, Samuele Scherini, Franca Garzotto
- **Abstract**: To combat climate change, individuals are encouraged to adopt sustainable habits, in particular, with their household, optimizing their electrical consumption. Conversational agents, such as Smart Home Assistants, hold promise as effective tools for promoting sustainable practices within households. Our research investigated the application of Large Language Models (LLM) in enhancing smart home automation and promoting sustainable household practices, specifically using the HomeAssistant framework. In particular, it highlights the potential of GPT models in generating accurate automation routines. While the LLMs showed proficiency in understanding complex commands and creating valid JSON outputs, challenges such as syntax errors and message malformations were noted, indicating areas for further improvement. Still, despite minimal quantitative differences between "green" and "no green" prompts, qualitative feedback highlighted a positive shift towards sustainability in the routines generated with environmentally focused prompts. Then, an empirical evaluation (N=56) demonstrated that the system was well-received and found engaging by users compared to its traditional rule-based counterpart. Our findings highlight the role of LLMs in advancing smart home technologies and suggest further research to refine these models for broader, real-world applications to support sustainable living.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate HomeAssistant automations with the goal of promoting sustainable energy practices in smart homes. The authors evaluate several LLMs (GPT-3.5, GPT-4, LLAMA2, MISTRAL, codeLLAMA) for their ability to generate valid HomeAssistant configuration files (JSON) from natural language user commands. They compare "green" prompts (explicitly promoting sustainability) to "no green" prompts to see if LLMs can generate more eco-conscious automations.  The authors then built EcoMate, a chatbot interface leveraging GPT-3.5, and compared its usability and user engagement against a traditional rule-based chatbot. The findings suggest that GPT models, particularly GPT-4, are proficient in generating HomeAssistant routines. However, LLMs show challenges with JSON formatting and interpreting certain user commands. Although "green" prompts did not always drastically alter the generated code, they did shift behavior towards sustainability attributes and faced higher hallucinations. The user evaluation revealed that EcoMate was well-received and engaging compared to a rule-based system.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies primarily in its specific application of LLMs within the *domain of home automation for sustainability* using the open-source HomeAssistant framework.  While LLMs have been used in smart home contexts before (King et al., as cited, is a direct predecessor), this work directly investigates and evaluates *generating automations aimed explicitly at sustainability* and benchmarks different LLMs. The user study comparing an LLM-based chatbot to a rule-based chatbot adds another layer, specifically looking at user perceptions and engagement in this context. However, the core concepts – using LLMs for code generation and conversational interfaces – are not entirely new.

**Significance:** The significance of the research resides in its potential to make smart home technology more accessible and effective for promoting sustainable living. By enabling users to create complex automations via natural language, it lowers the barrier to entry for optimizing energy consumption and related habits.  The empirical validation, though conducted in a controlled lab environment, offers initial evidence that LLM-powered interfaces can improve user engagement and satisfaction compared to more traditional methods.  The identification of the LLM's limitations (JSON validity, message malformation, hallucinations) is valuable for guiding future research and development in this area. However, the "green" prompt study showed minimal quantitative difference. The results suggest that further refinement in the methodology of generating routines that promote sustainability is necessary. It also focuses heavily on a single (albeit popular) framework, HomeAssistant, which limits generalizability. The study on LLM model training dataset consumption is missing from the current context. It is also worth considering if the benefits of using LLM offset its energy consumption (training+inference).

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Research Questions:** The paper defines focused research questions addressing both technical capabilities (LLM automation) and user perceptions.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper undertakes a thorough evaluation involving multiple LLMs, different prompt variations, and a user study.
*   **Practical Application:** The work is grounded in a real-world problem and utilizes a popular home automation framework.
*   **Open Science:** The data and code are made available, which supports reproducibility and further research.
*   **Identification of Limitations:** The paper acknowledges and analyzes the shortcomings of the LLMs.
*   **Well-Defined Methodology:** A structured methodology is employed for assessing various models.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Generalizability:** The study is highly specific to the HomeAssistant framework and a relatively small set of appliances.
*   **Lab Setting:**  The user study was conducted in a controlled lab environment, which may not fully reflect real-world usage scenarios and user behavior. The use of LLMs in a real-world setting with injected data also can affect the results.
*   **Modest Impact of "Green" Prompts:** The limited quantitative impact of the "green" prompts raises questions about how to effectively guide LLMs towards more sustainable outcomes. The lack of quantitative comparison to energy savings makes it hard to justify the adoption of such technology.
*   **Ethical Concerns**: There are some ethical questions that are not being addressed. For example, a biased LLM could favor groups with specific habits.
*   **Limited comparison between models**: There is a limited comparative analysis that measures the carbon footprint when adopting different models.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable exploration of LLMs for sustainable home automation using a systematic evaluation, but has room for improvement. It successfully identifies several key challenges and opportunities in this area. The weaknesses, especially the limited generalizability and the limited evidence of actual sustainability impact, temper the overall assessment.

Score: 6.5

### Towards Quantifying the Hessian Structure of Neural Networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02809v1)
- **Authors**: Zhaorui Dong, Yushun Zhang, Zhi-Quan Luo, Jianfeng Yao, Ruoyu Sun
- **Abstract**: Empirical studies reported that the Hessian matrix of neural networks (NNs) exhibits a near-block-diagonal structure, yet its theoretical foundation remains unclear. In this work, we reveal two forces that shape the Hessian structure: a ``static force'' rooted in the architecture design, and a ``dynamic force'' arisen from training. We then provide a rigorous theoretical analysis of ``static force'' at random initialization. We study linear models and 1-hidden-layer networks with the mean-square (MSE) loss and the Cross-Entropy (CE) loss for classification tasks. By leveraging random matrix theory, we compare the limit distributions of the diagonal and off-diagonal Hessian blocks and find that the block-diagonal structure arises as $C \rightarrow \infty$, where $C$ denotes the number of classes. Our findings reveal that $C$ is a primary driver of the near-block-diagonal structure. These results may shed new light on the Hessian structure of large language models (LLMs), which typically operate with a large $C$ exceeding $10^4$ or $10^5$.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary**

The paper, "Towards Quantifying the Hessian Structure of Neural Networks," investigates the near-block-diagonal structure observed in the Hessian matrices of neural networks. The authors propose that this structure arises from two primary forces: a "static force" stemming from the network's architecture and a "dynamic force" that emerges during training. They provide a rigorous theoretical analysis of the static force at random initialization, focusing on linear models and 1-hidden-layer networks with MSE and Cross-Entropy loss functions.  Using random matrix theory, they demonstrate that as the number of classes (C) increases, the off-diagonal blocks of the Hessian are suppressed, leading to a near-block-diagonal structure. The paper challenges the existing understanding that CE loss is the primary determining factor, arguing instead that the number of classes (C) plays a critical role. The authors also extend the analysis to 1-hidden-layer networks and highlight the technical challenges in characterizing the limit distribution of non-independent random matrix products.

**Critical Evaluation**

**Novelty:** The paper offers a valuable contribution by shifting the focus from CE loss to the number of classes (C) as a key driver of the near-block-diagonal Hessian structure. While empirical observations of this structure have been reported, the authors provide the first rigorous theoretical analysis, leveraging random matrix theory. The identification of "static" and "dynamic" forces as underlying factors provides a useful conceptual framework. The systematic procedure proposed to address diminishing dependencies due to ReLU and CE loss is a novel technical contribution. The extension of the analysis to 1-hidden-layer networks adds further value.

**Significance:** Understanding the Hessian structure has important implications for:

*   **NN training:** It can inform the design and effectiveness of optimization methods (e.g., Adam, Shampoo).
*   **New training method design:** It provides insights for developing more efficient and specialized optimizers.
*   **Optimization community**: Providing a new specialized class of problems to study.

By providing a theoretical foundation for the near-block-diagonal structure, the paper potentially opens new avenues for research in these areas. The findings are particularly relevant to large language models (LLMs) due to the models' extremely high number of classes.

**Strengths:**

*   **Rigorous Theoretical Analysis:** The paper provides a strong mathematical foundation, using random matrix theory to analyze the Hessian structure.
*   **Clear Identification of Forces:** The distinction between "static" and "dynamic" forces is helpful for understanding the complex interplay of factors influencing the Hessian.
*   **Relevance to LLMs:** The paper’s findings directly address a timely problem in the area of LLMs by providing an alternate point of view to the existing literature.
*   **Challenging Existing Wisdom:** It challenges the established view attributing the structure solely to CE loss, potentially leading to a more nuanced understanding.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Model Complexity:** The theoretical analysis primarily focuses on linear models and 1-hidden-layer networks. While providing a crucial first step, the generalizability to deeper and more complex architectures remains an open question.
*   **Idealized Assumptions:** The reliance on i.i.d. Gaussian data and LeCun initialization might limit the applicability to real-world scenarios with more complex data distributions and initialization schemes.
*   **Limited Discussion of Dynamic Force:** A more detailed analytical analysis of the "dynamic force" beyond the initial analysis in the appendix would further strengthen the paper.
*   **Future Work:** While the paper introduces insightful findings, extending the analysis to cover the whole training process and beyond initialization would considerably enhance its scope and impact.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper delivers a solid theoretical contribution to a relevant and challenging problem. While limited in model complexity, the rigorous analysis and the shift in perspective regarding the role of the number of classes provide valuable insights. The potential impact on LLM research is noteworthy. It is a good theoretical work with practical implications, but it also leaves questions open for future exploration.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and rigorous analysis of the Hessian structure, challenging existing assumptions and providing a valuable new perspective. However, the limited model complexity and idealistic assumption detract slightly from the significance. While promising, there is further research to be done.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Database-Agnostic Gait Enrollment using SetTransformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02815v1)
- **Authors**: Nicoleta Basoc, Adrian Cosma, Andy Cǎtrunǎ, Emilian Rǎdoi
- **Abstract**: Gait recognition has emerged as a powerful tool for unobtrusive and long-range identity analysis, with growing relevance in surveillance and monitoring applications. Although recent advances in deep learning and large-scale datasets have enabled highly accurate recognition under closed-set conditions, real-world deployment demands open-set gait enrollment, which means determining whether a new gait sample corresponds to a known identity or represents a previously unseen individual. In this work, we introduce a transformer-based framework for open-set gait enrollment that is both dataset-agnostic and recognition-architecture-agnostic. Our method leverages a SetTransformer to make enrollment decisions based on the embedding of a probe sample and a context set drawn from the gallery, without requiring task-specific thresholds or retraining for new environments. By decoupling enrollment from the main recognition pipeline, our model is generalized across different datasets, gallery sizes, and identity distributions. We propose an evaluation protocol that uses existing datasets in different ratios of identities and walks per identity. We instantiate our method using skeleton-based gait representations and evaluate it on two benchmark datasets (CASIA-B and PsyMo), using embeddings from three state-of-the-art recognition models (GaitGraph, GaitFormer, and GaitPT). We show that our method is flexible, is able to accurately perform enrollment in different scenarios, and scales better with data compared to traditional approaches. We will make the code and dataset scenarios publicly available.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will analyze the paper "Database-Agnostic Gait Enrollment using SetTransformers" based on its content, novelty, and significance within the field of gait recognition.

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of open-set gait enrollment, which is determining whether a new gait sample belongs to a known identity (present in the gallery) or represents a new person that needs to be enrolled. The authors propose a novel approach using a SetTransformer-based model that is both dataset-agnostic and recognition-architecture-agnostic. The SetTransformer makes enrollment decisions based on the embedding of a probe sample and a context set from the gallery, without relying on task-specific thresholds or retraining. The model is trained on diverse configurations of gallery-probe sets, ensuring generalization across different datasets, gallery sizes, and identity distributions. The authors evaluate their method on CASIA-B and PsyMo datasets using embeddings from GaitGraph, GaitFormer, and GaitPT models. They introduce an evaluation protocol with varying ratios of identities and walks per identity and demonstrate that their method performs well in different scenarios, scaling better than traditional approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the *dataset-agnostic* and *model-agnostic* approach to gait enrollment. While open-set gait recognition has been explored, most methods rely on threshold-based techniques or are tied to specific datasets and/or recognition architectures. Decoupling the enrollment process from the main recognition pipeline and training the model on varied gallery-probe configurations to enforce generalization are significant contributions. The use of SetTransformers is also a notable aspect, as it effectively handles the variable number of gallery embeddings and the need for permutation invariance. The evaluation protocol, with its focus on different identity-to-walk ratios, is also a valuable contribution towards simulating real-world scenarios.

*   **Significance:** Gait enrollment is a crucial aspect for the practical deployment of gait recognition systems. Existing methods often lack the flexibility and generalization ability needed for real-world applications. This research contributes by providing a more adaptable and scalable solution. The ability to leverage pre-trained embeddings from different gait recognition models allows for easy integration into existing systems. The dataset-agnostic nature eliminates the need for retraining when deploying in new environments. The demonstration on two well-known gait datasets and multiple recognition architectures further validates the significance and practicality of the approach. The release of the code and dataset scenarios further enhances the potential impact.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Novel and well-explained method using SetTransformers.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation protocol with diverse scenarios.
    *   Demonstrated generalization across datasets and recognition architectures.
    *   Release of code and dataset scenarios to promote reproducibility and adoption.
    *   Addresses an important and often overlooked aspect of gait recognition.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the evaluation is comprehensive, the results could be further strengthened by comparison with a broader range of existing open-set gait recognition methods, especially those utilizing radar data.
    *   The explanation of why certain gait embedding models generalize better for cross-dataset testing (e.g., GaitFormer) could be explored in more detail.
    *   The paper could delve deeper into the computational cost of the SetTransformer model and its scalability to very large galleries.
    *   The selection of K Nearest Neighbors (KNN) depends on d(p,gi), but the exact metric has not been mentioned.

*   **Potential Impact:** The research has the potential to significantly influence the development of more robust and adaptable gait recognition systems. The dataset-agnostic and model-agnostic nature of the approach makes it suitable for real-world applications where data distributions and system architectures may vary. This could lead to wider adoption of gait recognition technology in areas such as surveillance, access control, and person re-identification.

**Score:** 8

**Justification:** The paper introduces a novel and well-executed approach to gait enrollment that addresses a critical gap in the field. The dataset-agnostic and model-agnostic properties, coupled with the comprehensive evaluation protocol, demonstrate the practical significance of the work. While there are minor limitations regarding comparisons with existing methods and further analysis of the results, the overall contribution is substantial and likely to have a significant impact on the development of real-world gait recognition systems.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### ReplaceMe: Network Simplification via Layer Pruning and Linear Transformations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02819v1)
- **Authors**: Dmitriy Shopkhoev, Ammar Ali, Magauiya Zhussip, Valentin Malykh, Stamatios Lefkimmiatis, Nikos Komodakis, Sergey Zagoruyko
- **Abstract**: We introduce ReplaceMe, a generalized training-free depth pruning method that effectively replaces transformer blocks with a linear operation, while maintaining high performance for low compression ratios. In contrast to conventional pruning approaches that require additional training or fine-tuning, our approach requires only a small calibration dataset that is used to estimate a linear transformation to approximate the pruned blocks. This estimated linear mapping can be seamlessly merged with the remaining transformer blocks, eliminating the need for any additional network parameters. Our experiments show that ReplaceMe consistently outperforms other training-free approaches and remains highly competitive with state-of-the-art pruning methods that involve extensive retraining/fine-tuning and architectural modifications. Applied to several large language models (LLMs), ReplaceMe achieves up to 25% pruning while retaining approximately 90% of the original model's performance on open benchmarks - without any training or healing steps, resulting in minimal computational overhead (see Fig.1). We provide an open-source library implementing ReplaceMe alongside several state-of-the-art depth pruning techniques, available at this repository.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ReplaceMe: Network Simplification via Layer Pruning and Linear Transformations":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "ReplaceMe," a training-free depth pruning method for large language models (LLMs). ReplaceMe simplifies networks by replacing contiguous transformer blocks with a learned linear transformation (LT).  The method estimates this LT using a small calibration dataset. A key feature is the seamless integration of the LT into the remaining network, eliminating the need for additional parameters. Experiments show that ReplaceMe achieves significant pruning (up to 25%) while maintaining high performance on open benchmarks, outperforming other training-free approaches and remaining competitive with training-intensive pruning methods. The authors also provide an open-source library with their implementation and several state-of-the-art depth pruning techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using a *training-free* approach to depth pruning is not entirely new. However, the specific method of replacing transformer blocks with an analytically or numerically derived linear transformation, seamlessly integrated into the architecture without adding parameters, does exhibit some novelty. The authors' systematic study of different distance metrics, regularization techniques, and calibration data ablations further strengthens the contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper's primary significance lies in its practicality.  The training-free aspect makes it accessible and attractive for compressing large models when computational resources for fine-tuning are limited. Achieving competitive performance without the overhead of retraining/fine-tuning is a valuable contribution. The detailed ablation studies provide insights for practitioners looking to implement similar pruning strategies. The open-source library contributes to reproducibility and wider adoption.  The application of the approach to both LLMs and vision transformers also increases the potential impact.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Truly training-free. This is a major advantage in terms of computational cost.
    *   Effective: Achieves good performance compared to methods that require training.
    *   Seamless integration:  No additional parameters are introduced.
    *   Detailed empirical evaluation: Thorough ablation studies provide valuable insights.
    *   Open-source implementation: Facilitates reproducibility and adoption.
    *   The comparison with UIDL, showing better accuracy without needing training is a plus.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Performance limitations at high compression ratios: As the paper notes, a healing process becomes necessary at extreme compression levels. While the approach performs well at lower compression ratios, the lack of ability to significantly prune at higher levels and maintain performance is a limitation.
    *   Limited Generalization: While the method shows promises, the reliance on calibration data means that performance is inherently tied to the quality and representativeness of this dataset. Results in Table 3 demonstrate how instruction fine-tuned and plain text data can significantly impact accuracy, and even downstream benchmarking. As a result, this method does not truly replace all training and fine-tuning procedures.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence research and practice in model compression, particularly in scenarios where training resources are scarce or time is a constraint. The simplicity and accessibility of the method make it a valuable tool for deploying LLMs in resource-constrained environments. However, more aggressive pruning methods may be needed for some applications.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a practical and effective approach to training-free depth pruning with strong experimental results. The method fills an important niche in the model compression landscape. While the approach's effectiveness wanes as pruning becomes more aggressive, and generalization can be affected by the quality of the calibration dataset, the method's simplicity and effectiveness at low compression ratios make it a worthwhile contribution to the field.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The score reflects the paper's practical significance and effectiveness, countered by its limitations at high compression rates and its reliance on calibration datasets. While the core idea of using a linear transformation isn't revolutionary, the specific method and its effective integration within the network, coupled with detailed experimentation and an open-source implementation, warrant a relatively high score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Towards Dataset Copyright Evasion Attack against Personalized Text-to-Image Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02824v1)
- **Authors**: Kuofeng Gao, Yufei Zhu, Yiming Li, Jiawang Bai, Yong Yang, Zhifeng Li, Shu-Tao Xia
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models have rapidly advanced, enabling high-quality image generation conditioned on textual prompts. However, the growing trend of fine-tuning pre-trained models for personalization raises serious concerns about unauthorized dataset usage. To combat this, dataset ownership verification (DOV) has emerged as a solution, embedding watermarks into the fine-tuning datasets using backdoor techniques. These watermarks remain inactive under benign samples but produce owner-specified outputs when triggered. Despite the promise of DOV for T2I diffusion models, its robustness against copyright evasion attacks (CEA) remains unexplored. In this paper, we explore how attackers can bypass these mechanisms through CEA, allowing models to circumvent watermarks even when trained on watermarked datasets. We propose the first copyright evasion attack (i.e., CEAT2I) specifically designed to undermine DOV in T2I diffusion models. Concretely, our CEAT2I comprises three stages: watermarked sample detection, trigger identification, and efficient watermark mitigation. A key insight driving our approach is that T2I models exhibit faster convergence on watermarked samples during the fine-tuning, evident through intermediate feature deviation. Leveraging this, CEAT2I can reliably detect the watermarked samples. Then, we iteratively ablate tokens from the prompts of detected watermarked samples and monitor shifts in intermediate features to pinpoint the exact trigger tokens. Finally, we adopt a closed-form concept erasure method to remove the injected watermark. Extensive experiments show that our CEAT2I effectively evades DOV mechanisms while preserving model performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the growing concern of unauthorized dataset usage in the context of personalized text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models. It focuses on bypassing dataset ownership verification (DOV) mechanisms that embed watermarks in datasets using backdoor techniques. The paper identifies limitations in existing backdoor removal methods like TPD and T2IShield and proposes a novel copyright evasion attack called CEAT2I. CEAT2I comprises three stages: watermarked sample detection (based on convergence analysis), trigger identification (through token ablation), and efficient watermark mitigation (using a closed-form concept erasure method). The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of CEAT2I in evading DOV mechanisms across various datasets and DOV techniques while preserving model performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach to copyright evasion in T2I diffusion models. While backdoor attacks and defenses have been explored in other domains, the application and adaptation of such techniques specifically for T2I DOV, along with the proposed CEAT2I method, represent a new contribution. The key novelty lies in:

1.  **Problem Framing:** Clearly defining the copyright evasion attack problem in the context of T2I DOV, which hadn't been explicitly addressed before.
2.  **Convergence Analysis:** Using convergence analysis during fine-tuning as a reliable indicator for detecting watermarked samples is a novel insight. Identifying and exploiting the faster learning dynamics of watermarked data during fine-tuning is key.
3.  **CEAT2I Pipeline:** The integrated three-stage pipeline (detection, identification, and mitigation), especially the use of token ablation for trigger identification and closed-form concept erasure, offers a practical and effective solution.

**Significance:** The paper is significant because it highlights a critical vulnerability in DOV mechanisms for T2I models. The ease with which CEAT2I can bypass existing protections raises serious concerns about the practical effectiveness of current DOV techniques. This has implications for:

1.  **Copyright Protection:** Undermines the effectiveness of existing DOV approaches used by artists and organizations to protect their datasets and intellectual property.
2.  **Ethical AI Development:** Points to the need for more robust and resilient DOV methods to ensure responsible data usage in AI model training.
3.  **Research Direction:** The paper serves as a critical red-teaming exercise and opens up new research directions in developing more secure and robust DOV techniques.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of copyright evasion in T2I DOV.
*   **Thorough Analysis:** It provides a comprehensive analysis of existing backdoor removal techniques and their limitations in the T2I context.
*   **Effective Solution:** CEAT2I demonstrates strong performance in evading DOV mechanisms while maintaining model quality.
*   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experiments are well-designed and cover a range of DOV methods, datasets, and evaluation metrics.
*   **Ablation Studies:** The paper includes ablation studies to analyze the impact of different components of CEAT2I.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of DOV Methods:**  The paper primarily evaluates against backdoor-based watermarking DOV methods. While this is a common approach, it doesn't consider alternative DOV techniques that might exist.
*   **Specific T2I Architectures:**  CEAT2I is designed and evaluated for Stable Diffusion. Its effectiveness against other T2I architectures (e.g., transformer-based models) is not fully explored. Generalizability might be limited.
*   **Potential for Adaptive Defenses:** While the paper touches on adaptive defenses, it only tests against a specific type of defensive adaptation of the trigger. More comprehensive evaluations against a wider range of adaptive defenses are warranted.
*   **Computational Cost:** The paper acknowledges the extra computational overhead introduced by the watermark removal process, without providing a quantitative evaluation or detailed discussion on optimization strategies.

**Justification for the Score:**

Given the novelty in framing the problem, the insightful convergence analysis for detection, the effective CEAT2I method, the thorough experiments, and the overall significance to the field, the paper deserves a high score. However, some limitations exist in terms of generalizability to other architectures and a more thorough evaluation against more adaptive defenses that prevent detection of watermarked samples..

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### No Other Representation Component Is Needed: Diffusion Transformers Can Provide Representation Guidance by Themselves
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02831v1)
- **Authors**: Dengyang Jiang, Mengmeng Wang, Liuzhuozheng Li, Lei Zhang, Haoyu Wang, Wei Wei, Guang Dai, Yanning Zhang, Jingdong Wang
- **Abstract**: Recent studies have demonstrated that learning a meaningful internal representation can both accelerate generative training and enhance generation quality of the diffusion transformers. However, existing approaches necessitate to either introduce an additional and complex representation training framework or rely on a large-scale, pre-trained representation foundation model to provide representation guidance during the original generative training process. In this study, we posit that the unique discriminative process inherent to diffusion transformers enables them to offer such guidance without requiring external representation components. We therefore propose Self-Representation A}lignment (SRA), a simple yet straightforward method that obtain representation guidance through a self-distillation manner. Specifically, SRA aligns the output latent representation of the diffusion transformer in earlier layer with higher noise to that in later layer with lower noise to progressively enhance the overall representation learning during only generative training process. Experimental results indicate that applying SRA to DiTs and SiTs yields consistent performance improvements. Moreover, SRA not only significantly outperforms approaches relying on auxiliary, complex representation training frameworks but also achieves performance comparable to methods that heavily dependent on powerful external representation priors.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "No Other Representation Component Is Needed: Diffusion Transformers Can Provide Representation Guidance by Themselves" proposes a novel method called Self-Representation Alignment (SRA) to improve the training of diffusion transformers (DiTs and SiTs) without relying on external representation models or complex auxiliary training frameworks. SRA aligns the latent representations within the diffusion transformer itself by matching earlier layers (higher noise) with later layers (lower noise) through a self-distillation process. This aligns the features in the latent space of the diffusion model itself. The authors demonstrate that SRA consistently improves the performance of DiTs and SiTs, achieving results comparable to methods that use external representation priors. The method is simple, flexible, and resource-friendly.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of leveraging the diffusion transformer's inherent denoising process to provide its own representation guidance is moderately novel. While self-distillation and aligning features across different layers exist in other contexts, applying it specifically to the time series inherent within diffusion models *is* a fresh take. The paper provides strong empirical evidence to support its claims. This contrasts with existing approaches that require large pretrained models or auxiliary training tasks. The exploration of internal representations within diffusion models and their ability to guide training is a valuable contribution.

* **Significance:** The significance lies in the simplicity and effectiveness of the approach. The results demonstrate that a diffusion transformer, *on its own*, possesses sufficient internal representation capacity to improve generative training. This is impactful because it reduces the reliance on external resources and complexity, making it easier to train and deploy diffusion models. It also challenges the common paradigm of requiring extensive pre-training or external guidance. The improvement in performance compared to methods that use representation tasks is significant, as it demonstrates that this approach can be more effective.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Simplicity:** SRA is conceptually straightforward and easy to implement.
    * **Effectiveness:** The empirical results are strong and consistently demonstrate performance gains across different model sizes and architectures.
    * **Resource-Friendly:** SRA doesn't require large-scale pre-training or extra training data, making it accessible to researchers with limited computational resources.
    * **Thorough Evaluation:** The paper includes a comprehensive set of experiments, including component-wise analysis, comparisons with existing methods, and ablation studies.
    * **Clear Writing:**  The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Theoretical Justification:** While the authors hypothesize about the coarse-to-fine nature of diffusion models, a deeper theoretical analysis of why SRA works is lacking. More insight into why certain layers are better for alignment would be beneficial.
    * **Incremental Improvement:** Although the results are consistently positive, the performance gains over the baselines, while statistically significant, are incremental. The improvement is solid, but not revolutionary.
    * **Dependence on EMA Teacher:** The paper relies on an EMA teacher network to ensure stable training, this might require fine-tuning of momentum parameter.

* **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence future research by encouraging the exploration of self-supervised techniques within diffusion models. It may also lead to the development of more efficient and resource-friendly training methods for generative models.

* **Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a moderately novel and effective approach to training diffusion transformers.  While the performance gains are incremental and the theoretical justification is somewhat limited, the simplicity and resource-friendliness of SRA make it a valuable contribution to the field.  The comprehensive evaluation and clear writing further strengthen the paper. Therefore, a score of 7 reflects the paper's significant but not groundbreaking impact. The work is well-executed and provides a solid foundation for further research in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Unlearning vs. Obfuscation: Are We Truly Removing Knowledge?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02884v1)
- **Authors**: Guangzhi Sun, Potsawee Manakul, Xiao Zhan, Mark Gales
- **Abstract**: Unlearning has emerged as a critical capability for large language models (LLMs) to support data privacy, regulatory compliance, and ethical AI deployment. Recent techniques often rely on obfuscation by injecting incorrect or irrelevant information to suppress knowledge. Such methods effectively constitute knowledge addition rather than true removal, often leaving models vulnerable to probing. In this paper, we formally distinguish unlearning from obfuscation and introduce a probing-based evaluation framework to assess whether existing approaches genuinely remove targeted information. Moreover, we propose DF-MCQ, a novel unlearning method that flattens the model predictive distribution over automatically generated multiple-choice questions using KL-divergence, effectively removing knowledge about target individuals and triggering appropriate refusal behaviour. Experimental results demonstrate that DF-MCQ achieves unlearning with over 90% refusal rate and a random choice-level uncertainty that is much higher than obfuscation on probing questions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the critical problem of "unlearning" in large language models (LLMs), distinguishing it from "obfuscation." The authors argue that many current unlearning techniques are essentially obfuscation methods that add irrelevant or incorrect information rather than truly removing knowledge. They formally define unlearning and obfuscation, and introduce a novel evaluation framework using automatically generated probing questions (open-ended, Yes-No, multiple-choice) to assess whether unlearning is truly achieved. Furthermore, they propose a new unlearning method called DF-MCQ (Distribution Flattening Multiple-Choice Question), which flattens the model's predictive distribution over automatically generated multiple-choice questions using KL-divergence. Experimental results demonstrate that DF-MCQ achieves high unlearning effectiveness with high refusal rates and improved uncertainty on probing questions, outperforming obfuscation-based methods like WHP+.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper tackles an important and timely problem: the need for true unlearning in LLMs, beyond merely obfuscating existing knowledge.  The distinction between unlearning and obfuscation is a valuable contribution to the field, providing a more nuanced understanding of what it means to remove information from a model.

*   **Novelty:**  The formal definition of unlearning versus obfuscation and the probing-based evaluation framework are both novel and significant.  The DF-MCQ method itself represents a novel approach, leveraging distribution flattening with multiple-choice questions. The use of KL-divergence for flattening the predictive distribution in MCQs to eliminate connections is a unique aspect of their approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper highlights the limitations of existing obfuscation-based methods and offers a more principled approach to knowledge removal.  The proposed evaluation framework has the potential to become a standard tool for assessing unlearning techniques. The DF-MCQ method shows promising results, particularly in generating appropriate refusal behaviors, which is a crucial aspect of trustworthy LLMs. The experimental results clearly highlight the drawbacks of obfuscation-based techniques in the face of specific probing question designs. The generalization of DF-MCQ to another foundation model adds credibility to its design.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and concise definitions of unlearning and obfuscation.
    *   Rigorous evaluation framework using diverse probing questions.
    *   Effective unlearning method (DF-MCQ) with strong experimental results.
    *   Demonstrates the limitations of obfuscation-based methods.
    *   Code availability enhances reproducibility and adoption.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The evaluation is primarily focused on person-centric facts within the WPU dataset. While important, it doesn't cover all types of knowledge or contexts.
    *   The DF-MCQ method relies on automatic question generation, which could introduce biases or inconsistencies. The quality of generated MCQs is key and requires further scrutiny. Although well documented, it would strengthen the study to investigate the limitations of the probing methodology.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to shift the focus of unlearning research towards true knowledge removal rather than obfuscation.  The probing-based evaluation framework could become a standard tool in the field. DF-MCQ provides a promising alternative approach that could inspire further research on distribution flattening techniques for unlearning. The authors address an important issue with current unlearning methods: that they are actually obfuscation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by clearly defining and differentiating between unlearning and obfuscation, and providing a novel evaluation framework. The DF-MCQ method is a valuable addition to the existing unlearning techniques. However, the relatively narrow scope of the experiments (person-centric data) and the reliance on automatically generated data prevent it from achieving a higher score. The paper offers a path forward, but more comprehensive validation is needed.
Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### When Your Own Output Becomes Your Training Data: Noise-to-Meaning Loops and a Formal RSI Trigger
